HIS CENTRAL POINT: raising this paper requires CHANGING ITS MATHEMATICAL CENTRE. Another
two-state quotient, image equation, fibre parametrization, covariance formula or
model-specific test will not do it - those directions are capped by existing order-two
theory. Meszaros-Telek already give a canonical representation of the whole order-two
DRAP/DMAP class through four process-level parameters, and Ramirez-Cobo, Lillo and Wiper
give a complete characterization of weakly equivalent MAP2 processes. Do not spend effort
inside that capped region.

DO TARGET 2 FIRST, THEN TARGET 1. He ranks target 2 at 85% success but only 4.5/10 impact,
calling it an important repair and a PREREQUISITE; target 1 at 60% x 9.0 = 5.40, "the only
target with a realistic chance of raising the paper a full tier". So they are sequential,
not alternatives.

TARGET 2 (prerequisite): the complete stationary singular limit experiment for the PRESENT
two-state model. Prove it properly - uniform stationary record LAN, the singular/double-pole
structure, and the associated testing statement - for the model the paper already has. He
says this repairs an important gap. It will not raise the tier by itself; it is the
foundation for target 1.

TARGET 1 (the tier-raising result): a uniform stationary-record singular limit experiment,
with efficient testing and a MATCHING N^{-1/4} minimax rate, for collisions of rates in
sampled generalized-Erlang / serial phase-type renewal processes - that is, sampled
generalized-Erlang / serial DPH(n) records. This moves the paper from a highly explicit
analysis of one constrained D-MAP(2) to a theorem about a STANDARD PHASE-TYPE FAMILY and a
genuine nonregular statistical phenomenon.

His 60% estimate is conditional on the theorem being restricted to: fixed n; one isolated
double collision; all other rates uniformly separated; compact positive rate sets; and any
further conditions he lists - read them from his report and adopt them exactly. Do NOT try to
prove it in greater generality on this pass; the restricted theorem at 60% is the target, and
a general version is not what was costed.

WHAT ALREADY FEEDS THIS, from the current manuscript: the stopped batch-means argument, which
exposes the balance b_{N-1} + b_N/N and yields N^{-1/3}; the physical-image proof with its
uniform tail and covariance-nondegeneracy arguments; and Theorem D, the exact stationary-record
likelihood factorization for the Markov-gap path. Read these before starting and say which
ones carry over.

Note the rate change: the existing argument yields N^{-1/3}; the target is a matching
N^{-1/4} minimax rate at the double collision, with separation theta_{1,N} - theta_{2,N} of
order N^{-1/4}. Getting the rate right is the substance, not a detail.

DO NOT PURSUE the complete order-three DPH canonical representation. He puts it at 8% and
says the current machinery is structurally inadequate.

HONEST OUTCOMES: if target 1 does not close, say so in the FIRST LINE of your report, give
the exact step that fails, and report target 2 on its own merits. A sharp obstruction is a

That is a direct completion, with high success probability. Its weakness is priority and scope, not feasibility.

2.3 A tempting named-open target that is not reachable
A genuinely major representation-theoretic target would be:
Complete order-three DPH canonical representation theorem
Every minimal DPH distribution of order three admits, according to its real/complex eigenvalue type, a representative in a finite explicit list of Markovian canonical zero patterns; the representative is unique modulo the prescribed state permutations; and explicit necessary-and-sufficient semialgebraic inequalities and a terminating inversion algorithm decide which pattern applies.
Papp and Telek explicitly state that general cyclic DPH canonical representations were unavailable and that full characterization of the order-three class required completing their Theorem 9. Roundcube Webmail+1
This would have very high impact. It is not reachable from the present paper. The current machinery supplies:


a serial positive-real-eigenvalue subclass;


local similarity-orbit geometry at strictly positive minimal kernels;


one explicit order-two cone intersection.


It does not supply:


global boundary stratification;


negative or complex spectral cases;


a finite exhaustive list of order-three Markovian forms;


global semialgebraic existence and uniqueness;


control of nonminimal degenerations.


The manuscript itself identifies these as open interfaces rather than consequences.  Reaching the DPH3 target would require new invariant-cone and semialgebraic geometry, not a longer application of the existing similarity argument.

4. Which tier-raising levers apply?
(a) Settling a named open problem in the literature: does not apply

Two explicit nearby benchmarks illustrate the mismatch.
First, the order-three DPH canonical problem is relevant to phase-type representation, but the paper has only local interior orbit geometry and a serial subclass; it lacks the global boundary and spectral analysis needed for that problem. Roundcube Webmail+1
Second, Asanjarani and Nazarathy formulate the conjectures that every irreducible MMPP has squared coefficient of variation at least one and satisfies a specified stochastic order. å‰‘æ¡¥å¤§å­¦å‡ºç‰ˆç¤¾ These concern nonrenewal Markov-modulated Poisson processes of arbitrary order. The deterministic-reset renewal reduction in this manuscript does not address them.
Resolution condition: lever (a) would become available only after importing a fundamentally different global representation or MMPP argument. It is not a responsible target for the present project.

(b) Proving something about the fieldâ€™s standard objects: applies, and is essential
The target should be stated for:

sampled generalized-Erlang distributions, equivalently serial DPH(n) killed-reset renewal records with one repeated rate.

Generalized-Erlang/Coxian and phase-type distributions are standard objects. The present paper already invokes their representation theory, but only to obtain a finite-coordinate population-identifiability corollary. 
The new result must be advertised as a theorem about singular statistical inference for sampled generalized-Erlang renewal processes. The two-state killed-reset counter should become the n=2 worked example.
This lever is the main source of the proposed theoremâ€™s tier impact. Without it, even a perfect stationary n=2 LAN theorem remains too model-local.

(c) Removing a hypothesis: applies in two precise ways, but not to the reset condition
Hypothesis removal 1: deterministic product sample â†’ stationary record
The current singular calculation assumes
mNâ€‹(z)=âŒŠN/Î¼zâ€‹âŒ‹
independent complete gaps. The paper expressly says this is not a stationary-record endpoint equivalence.  Removing that sampling idealization is mathematically meaningful because the observed object throughout the paper is the stationary binary record.
Hypothesis removal 2: two phases â†’ arbitrary fixed serial order
The present symmetric-split calculation is two-rate. Extending the theorem to one isolated collision in an arbitrary fixed generalized-Erlang order makes the phenomenon representation-theoretic rather than device-specific.
Hypothesis that should not be advertised as removable: deterministic post-click reset
The manuscript correctly proves that allowing a positive marked transition into R destroys the fixed Palm restart and invalidates the renewal product, covariance transfer, and quotient route in general.  Removing that hypothesis would require hidden-Markov or Markov-renewal likelihood theory and a different identifiability analysis. It is not a strengthening obtainable from the current proof.

This is the second essential lever.

The theorem should also state and attain the one-sided local testing envelope. That separates it from a mere Taylor expansion showing that h2 appears.
The moving-dimensional Helmert â€œif and only ifâ€ boundary, even if fully correct, is not the sharpness result I would choose as the paperâ€™s upgrade. It concerns the validity of one high-dimensional Gaussian approximation inside a supplementary construction. The collision lower/upper bound concerns the fundamental recoverability of a standard phase-type parameter and therefore has substantially wider priority value.

5. Strongest remaining higher-tier objection
Even after the proposed theorem is proved, the hardest credible referee objection would be:

â€œAfter reparametrizing by squared root separation, the new result is an application of ordinary one-sided LAN to a smooth renewal family. The rest of the manuscript is still an overgrown union of explicit DMAP2 algebra, weak-equivalence geometry, specification tests, semiparametric Markov alternatives, growing-dimensional Gaussian coupling, and a projection example. The paper has not demonstrated that its flagship theorem is either general enough or central enough to justify this architecture.â€

That objection would be serious. General phase-type Fisher-information calculations already exist, regular dead-time event-detection LAN and efficient estimators now exist, and generic boundary and loss-of-identifiability theory is standard. Welcome to DTU Research Database+2arXiv+2 The paper therefore cannot claim priority merely for placing familiar boundary asymptotics into new notation.
What would resolve it
1. Make the n-phase theorem, not the two-state corollary, the headline
The paper must prove the pole-order nondegeneracy uniformly for an isolated double pole in arbitrary fixed serial order. That is the part not supplied automatically by generic boundary theory.
2. Include the complete stationary experiment
The theorem must cover the actual stationary binary record, including random renewal stopping and endpoint censoring. A proof only for independent complete gaps will not suffice.
3. Include both optimal testing and matching estimation rates
A LAN display alone is not enough. The paper needs:


efficient nuisance residualization;


the exact one-sided local power envelope;


a contiguity-based Nâˆ’1/4 lower bound;


an explicit rate-attaining estimator.


4. Rebuild the article around one proof chain
A credible higher-tier organization would be:


sampled generalized-Erlang renewal model and quotient collision coordinates;


analytic continuation and sampled pole-order nondegeneracy;


stationary likelihood and uniform LAN;


Gaussian cone experiment and efficient testing;


sharp estimation rate;


two-state killed-reset counter as an explicit corollary.


5. Remove or separate material that does not serve that chain
From the main paper I would move out, or delete:


the complete-visible-law distributional-distance test;


the physical-image specification test;


the fixed-marginal Markov tangent;


the moving Helmert layer;


the infinite weighted score test;


the pointwise projection example;


the general (nâˆ’1)2 similarity-orbit discussion.


The exact two-state fibre arc could remain as a short population contrast, but it should not compete with the singular-inference theorem for headline status. The present comparison table already concedes that the arc is a cone-intersection corollary of weak-equivalence theory and that the higher-state dimension statement is only a local Lie-orbit result.  
6. Finish the canonical-coordinate priority reduction
The three-inclusion quotient must either be explicitly derived from the MÃ©szÃ¡rosâ€“Telek coordinates and labelled as a useful specialization, or be shown to contain a genuinely new minimal-observation statement not present there. Until then it should not remain a coequal flagship.
7. Remove any physical-system implication not supported by validation
The current PDF correctly says that the sampled counter is an interpretation of the constrained kernel, not an independently analysed physical system.  A mathematically recentered article should maintain that restraint. A higher-tier physical or engineering claim would require a real detector model, simulation study, or experimental comparison beyond the present theorem package.

Final go/no-go assessment
Go on the n-phase stationary double-pole theorem, beginning with the n=2 stationary proof as an internal milestone.
No-go on trying to raise the tier through further two-state quotient formulas, additional image tests, longer fibre classifications inside the same constrained cone, more delta methods, or a polished proof of the existing product-gap split expansion. Those may improve correctness but do not alter the paperâ€™s priority class.
The structural ceiling of the current architecture is that deterministic reset converts the problem to a renewal distribution, while the population representation questions then reduce largely to standard order-two canonical coordinates, minimal-realization equivalence, or generalized-Erlang representation. The proposed stationary singular theorem escapes that ceiling because it asks a different and genuinely sharper question: what is the complete local statistical experiment, and the exact recoverability boundary, when a standard phase-type representation loses root identifiability through pole collision?

codex
The report’s admissible restriction list is exact: fixed \(n\), one isolated double collision, every rate in a compact subset \([\varepsilon,M]\subset(0,\infty)\), uniform separation \(c_0\) from the collision and between all remaining rates, and known sampling interval \(\Delta\). Its new required lemma is the sampled pole-order nondegeneracy statement: the collision derivative must contribute a nonzero \(k^3e^{-\lambda\Delta k}\) term that no nuisance derivative can cancel. I’m now locating the theorem registry, source split, baseline PDFs, and all verification entry points; I’ll also use the local PDF instructions for the required layout/page checks.

---
name: "pdf"
description: "Use when tasks involve reading, creating, or reviewing PDF files where rendering and layout matter; prefer visual checks by rendering pages (Poppler) and use Python tools such as `reportlab`, `pdfplumber`, and `pypdf` for generation and extraction."
---


# PDF Skill

## When to use
- Read or review PDF content where layout and visuals matter.
- Create PDFs programmatically with reliable formatting.
- Validate final rendering before delivery.

## Workflow
1. Prefer visual review: render PDF pages to PNGs and inspect them.
   - Use `pdftoppm` if available.
   - If unavailable, install Poppler or ask the user to review the output locally.
2. Use `reportlab` to generate PDFs when creating new documents.
3. Use `pdfplumber` (or `pypdf`) for text extraction and quick checks; do not rely on it for layout fidelity.
4. After each meaningful update, re-render pages and verify alignment, spacing, and legibility.

## Temp and output conventions
- Use `tmp/pdfs/` for intermediate files; delete when done.
- Write final artifacts under `output/pdf/` when working in this repo.
- Keep filenames stable and descriptive.

## Dependencies (install if missing)
Prefer `uv` for dependency management.

Python packages:
```
uv pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
If `uv` is unavailable:
```
python3 -m pip install reportlab pdfplumber pypdf
```
System tools (for rendering):
```
# macOS (Homebrew)
brew install poppler

# Ubuntu/Debian
sudo apt-get install -y poppler-utils
```

If installation isn't possible in this environment, tell the user which dependency is missing and how to install it locally.

## Environment
No required environment variables.

## Rendering command
```
pdftoppm -png $INPUT_PDF $OUTPUT_PREFIX
```

## Quality expectations
- Maintain polished visual design: consistent typography, spacing, margins, and section hierarchy.
- Avoid rendering issues: clipped text, overlapping elements, broken tables, black squares, or unreadable glyphs.
- Charts, tables, and images must be sharp, aligned, and clearly labeled.
- Use ASCII hyphens only. Avoid U+2011 (non-breaking hyphen) and other Unicode dashes.
- Citations and references must be human-readable; never leave tool tokens or placeholder strings.

## Final checks
- Do not deliver until the latest PNG inspection shows zero visual or formatting defects.
- Confirm headers/footers, page numbering, and section transitions look polished.
- Keep intermediate files organized or remove them after final approval.

\title{Supplement to ``Renewal laws, quotient inversion, and Markovian fibres for a killed-reset D-MAP''}
\author{Haobo Ma \and Wenlin Zhang}
\date{}

\begin{document}
\maketitle

This supplement contains the abstract inference layer supporting the article:
the stopped separated-batch covariance argument, the sampled-counter section
of the known-marginal Markov interaction tangent, the moving-dimensional
Helmert coupling boundary, the symmetric split likelihood experiment, and a
pointwise projection calculation.  Article labels are imported with
\texttt{xr-hyper}.  Computational checks are regression and formula sanity
checks only; none is used in place of an asymptotic remainder bound.

\section{Reproducibility scope}

The algebraic regression scripts are
\texttt{artifacts/verify\_A8\_results.py} and
\texttt{artifacts/verify\_nstate\_identifiability.py}; their unit-test
wrappers are the correspondingly named \texttt{test\_*.py} files.  The
fixed-point interval certificate is replayed by
\texttt{certificates/diagonal\_branch\_certificate.py --test}.  These checks
cover finite algebra and interval arithmetic.  They do not establish the
uniform limits in the preceding sections.

References cited in this supplement are listed in the article bibliography.

\begin{maintheorem}[Compact-uniform complete-Palm-gap split subexperiment]
\label{thm:singular-exchange-limit-experiment-main}
Let \(\mathcal K\Subset(0,1)\) and \(H<\infty\).  For
\(z\in\mathcal K\), let
\(m_N(z)=\lfloor N/\mu_z\rfloor\), and denote by \(P_{N,z,h}\) the product
law of \(m_N(z)\) complete Palm gaps with survival factors
\((p_{N,z,h},s_{N,z,h})\).  For \(|h|\le H\) and all sufficiently large
\(N\), put
\[
 p_{N,z,h}=z+hN^{-1/4},\qquad
 s_{N,z,h}=z-hN^{-1/4},\qquad
 P_{N,z,h}=P_{p_{N,z,h},s_{N,z,h}}^{(N)}.
\tag{SE1}
\]
At the diagonal let
\[
 D_z(k)=1-z+\log z\{kz-k+z\},\qquad
 g_z(k)=z^kD_z(k),\qquad k\ge0,
\tag{SE2}
\]
and define
\[
\begin{split}
B_z(k)=\frac{z^{k-2}}6\bigl[&
k^3(z-1)\log z+3k^2\{\log z+1-z\}\\
&-3kz\log z-6kz-2z\log z-3z\bigr],
\qquad b_z(k)=\frac{B_z(k)}{g_z(k)} .
\end{split}
\tag{SE3}
\]
Then \(g_z\) is the diagonal Palm gap mass and
\[
 g_{z+d,z-d}(k)=g_z(k)+d^2B_z(k)+O(d^4)
\tag{SE4}
\]
for every \(k\).  Moreover,
\[
 \sum_{k\ge0}g_z(k)b_z(k)=0,\qquad
 \mu_z=\sum_{k\ge0}(k+1)g_z(k)
 =\frac{1-z-z\log z}{(1-z)^2},
\tag{SE5}
\]
and
\[
 \mathcal I(z)=\frac1{\mu_z}
 \sum_{k\ge0}g_z(k)b_z(k)^2
 =\frac1{\mu_z}\sum_{k\ge0}\frac{B_z(k)^2}{g_z(k)}
\tag{SE6}
\]
is finite, strictly positive, and continuous on \((0,1)\).

Write the observed complete Palm gaps as
\(G_{1,N},\ldots,G_{m_N(z),N}\).  Set
\[
 \Delta_{N,z}=\frac1{\sqrt N}
 \sum_{j=1}^{m_N(z)}b_z(G_{j,N}).
\tag{SE7}
\]
Uniformly for \(z\in\mathcal K\),
\[
 \Delta_{N,z}\ \Longrightarrow\
 \mathcal N\{0,\mathcal I(z)\}
\tag{SE8}
\]
in bounded-Lipschitz distance under \(P_{N,z,0}\).  More precisely, for every
\(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\sup_{\substack{z\in\mathcal K\\ |h|\le H}}
P_{N,z,0}\!\left(
\left|
\log\frac{dP_{N,z,h}}{dP_{N,z,0}}
-h^2\Delta_{N,z}
+\frac12h^4\mathcal I(z)
\right|>\varepsilon
\right)\longrightarrow0 .
\tag{SE9}
\]
Consequently the likelihood-ratio processes, indexed by \(|h|\le H\),
converge uniformly over \(z\in\mathcal K\) to the scalar Gaussian experiments
\[
 X_z\sim\mathcal N\!\left(h^2\sqrt{\mathcal I(z)},1\right),
\qquad |h|\le H.
\tag{SE10}
\]
Equivalently, under \(P_{N,z,h}\),
\[
 \Delta_{N,z}\Longrightarrow
 \mathcal N\{h^2\mathcal I(z),\mathcal I(z)\}
\tag{SE11}
\]
uniformly over \(z\in\mathcal K\) and bounded \(h\).
The limit identifies \(h^2\), not the sign of \(h\).  This is the complete
likelihood limit for the specified symmetric split product subexperiment; it
does not claim stationary-record endpoint equivalence, exhaust all local
directions, or identify an ordered hidden label.
\end{maintheorem}

\begin{proof}
Write \(x=-\log p\), \(y=-\log s\).  The Palm gap formula in Theorem~A is
\[
 g_{p,s}(k)
 =\frac{y(1-p)p^k-x(1-s)s^k}{y-x},
\tag{SE12}
\]
with its continuous value when \(p=s\).  Substitute \(p=z+d\) and \(s=z-d\)
and expand the even analytic function of \(d\).  The constant and quadratic
coefficients are exactly \textup{(SE2)} and \textup{(SE3)}, proving
\textup{(SE4)}.  The diagonal survival tail is
\[
 S_z(k)=z^k(1-k\log z).
\]
Thus \(g_z(k)=S_z(k)-S_z(k+1)\), which proves the first identity in
\textup{(SE2)} and, by summing the tails, the formula for \(\mu_z\).
Differentiating \(\sum_kg_{z+d,z-d}(k)=1\) at order \(d^2\) gives
\(\sum_kB_z(k)=0\), hence the centring in \textup{(SE5)}.

On every \(\mathcal K\Subset(0,1)\), the masses in \textup{(SE12)} and all
their derivatives through any fixed order have a common
\(C(1+k)^m r^k\) envelope for some \(r<1\).  Termwise differentiation and
summation are therefore valid uniformly on \(\mathcal K\).  Formula
\textup{(SE3)} gives \(|b_z(k)|\le C_{\mathcal K}(1+k)^2\), so
\textup{(SE6)} is finite and continuous.  It is positive: if it vanished,
then \(B_z(k)=0\) for every \(k\).  But
\[
 B_z(0)=-\frac{2\log z+3}{6z};
\]
its only zero is \(z=e^{-3/2}\), and at that point the numerator in
\textup{(SE3)} for \(k=1\) equals \(-6z\), so \(B_z(1)\ne0\).

Put \(q=d^2\).  Uniform analytic differentiation in the symmetric coordinate
\((p+s,ps)=(2z,z^2-q)\) gives
\[
 g_{z+\sqrt q,z-\sqrt q}(k)
 =g_z(k)\{1+qb_z(k)+q^2c_z(k)+q^3R_{z,q}(k)\},
\tag{SE13}
\]
where \(\sum_kg_z(k)c_z(k)=0\) and \(c_z,R_{z,q}\) have common polynomial
envelopes on \(\mathcal K\) for \(q\) in a fixed right neighbourhood of zero.
The same statement follows directly from \textup{(SE12)}.  Uniform
exponential gap tails imply
\[
 \max_{1\le j\le m_N(z)}G_{j,N}=O_{P_{N,z,0}}(\log N)
\tag{SE14}
\]
uniformly in \(z\in\mathcal K\).  Taylor expansion of the logarithm in
\textup{(SE13)}, on the event in \textup{(SE14)}, therefore gives uniformly
for \(|h|\le H\), with \(q=h^2N^{-1/2}\),
\[
\begin{split}
\sum_{j=1}^{m_N(z)}\log
\frac{g_{z+\sqrt q,z-\sqrt q}(G_{j,N})}{g_z(G_{j,N})}
={}&h^2\Delta_{N,z}\\
&+\frac{h^4}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{m_N(z)}
\{c_z(G_{j,N})-\tfrac12b_z(G_{j,N})^2\}+o_P(1).
\end{split}
\tag{SE15}
\]

Under the diagonal law the complete Palm gaps are i.i.d. with mass \(g_z\),
and \(m_N(z)/N\to1/\mu_z\) uniformly on \(\mathcal K\).  The uniform law of
large numbers applied
to the two polynomially bounded rewards in \textup{(SE15)} makes its second
line converge to \(-h^4\mathcal I(z)/2\), uniformly on
\(\mathcal K\times[-H,H]\).  The uniform triangular-array central limit theorem
applied to \(b_z\) proves \textup{(SE8)}.  Both results can also be obtained by
the subsequence criterion: every sequence \(z_N\in\mathcal K\) has a
convergent subsequence, and the common exponential envelope supplies
Lindeberg and uniform integrability along that subsequence.  Because
\(P_{N,z,h}\) is the displayed product law, \textup{(SE15)} is its exact
log-likelihood ratio.  The uniform law of large numbers therefore proves
\textup{(SE9)} without an endpoint or Palm-inversion remainder.

The right side of \textup{(SE9)} is a continuous function of \(h\) and the
remainder is uniform on the compact index set.  Hence the likelihood-ratio
processes converge in \(C([-H,H])\) to
\[
 h\longmapsto
 \exp\{h^2\sqrt{\mathcal I(z)}\,Z-\tfrac12h^4\mathcal I(z)\},
 \qquad Z\sim\mathcal N(0,1),
\]
which is \textup{(SE10)}.  Le Cam's third lemma gives \textup{(SE11)}.
Finally,
\[
\sqrt N\{(p_{N,z,h}+s_{N,z,h},p_{N,z,h}s_{N,z,h})
-(2z,z^2)\}=(0,-h^2),
\]
which explains both the \(N^{-1/4}\) scale and the loss of the sign.  General
locally conic and loss-of-identifiability asymptotics are established in
\cite{DacunhaCastelleGassiat1997LocallyConic,
LiuShao2003LossIdentifiability}; the calculation above is the
compact-uniform complete-Palm-gap specialization.
\end{proof}



 succeeded in 177ms:
\section{Finite-Record Regenerative Tools}\label{app:finite-record-regenerative-tools-main}

This appendix collects the auxiliary renewal facts used by the article's
fixed-setting inclusion CLT.
The paper-level retained inclusion covariance estimator is proved in the
main-text adjacent-gap master theorem
\Cref{prop:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin-main}.  The fixed-range
facts below are auxiliary standard regenerative tools used only by secondary
diagnostics and supplementary protocols.

\begin{proposition}[Separated-batch covariance for fixed-range gap rewards]\label{thm:regenerative-separated-batch-covariance-main}
Fix an integer \(m\ge0\) and a dimension \(d\).  Let
\((G_j)_{j\in\mathbb Z}\) be i.i.d. with
\(\EE e^{\eta G_0}<\infty\) for some \(\eta>0\), and let
\[
Y_j=f(G_j,\ldots,G_{j+m})\in\RR^d,\qquad
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad \EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty .
\]
Set
\[
T_m=\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+
\sum_{h=1}^m\{\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_h)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_h,Y_0)\}.
\]
Let \(b_N\to\infty\), \(b_N/N\to0\),
\(M_N=\lfloor N/(b_N+m)\rfloor\), and define the separated batches
\[
I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)(b_N+m),\ldots,
(\ell-1)(b_N+m)+b_N-1\},\qquad
B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}Y_j ,
\]
for \(1\le\ell\le M_N\).  On \(M_N\ge2\), put
\(\bar B_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}B_{\ell,N}\).  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:regenerative-separated-batch-covariance-main}
\left\|
\frac{1}{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)
(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top
-T_m
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The separated blocks \(I_{\ell,N}\) are independent because the reward has
range \(m\).  Stationarity gives
\(b_N^{-1}\Var(B_{\ell,N})=T_m+O(b_N^{-1})\), and the fourth-moment expansion
for \(m\)-dependent sums gives \(\EE\|B_{\ell,N}\|^4\le Cb_N^2\).  Applying
Kolmogorov's maximal inequality entrywise to the independent centered matrices
\[
b_N^{-1}\{B_{\ell,N}B_{\ell,N}^\top-\EE(B_{\ell,N}B_{\ell,N}^\top)\}
\]
over the deterministic prefix \(M_N=\lfloor N/(b_N+m)\rfloor\) gives the
\(\sqrt{b_N/N}\) fluctuation term, and the empirical batch mean contributes
only \(O_{\PP}(M_N^{-1})\).  This proves
\eqref{eq:regenerative-separated-batch-covariance-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Calendar-stopped separated batches with plug-in centering]\label{prop:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin-main}
Let \((G_j)_{j\ge0}\) be i.i.d. nonnegative integer-valued gaps with
\(\EE e^{\eta G_0}<\infty\) for some \(\eta>0\), put
\(L_j=G_j+1\), \(\mu=\EE L_0\), and
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}L_j,\qquad
K_N=\max\{k:S_k<N\}.
\]
Fix \(m\ge0\), \(d\ge1\), and
\[
Y_j=f(G_j,\ldots,G_{j+m})\in\RR^d,\qquad
\EE Y_j=0,
\]
where
\[
\|f(g_0,\ldots,g_m)\|\le C_f\left(1+\sum_{i=0}^m g_i\right).
\]
Let
\[
\mathcal T_m=\Var(Y_0)+
\sum_{h=1}^{m}\{\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_h)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_h,Y_0)\}.
\]
For \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\), set
\(d_N=b_N+m\) and
\[
I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\},
\qquad
B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}Y_j .
\]
Fix an integer \(q\ge0\), representing the fixed terminal endpoint and
look-ahead deletion.  The all-complete-cycle stopped batch count is
\[
M_N=\max\{M\ge0:\ (M-1)d_N+b_N-1+m+q\le K_N-1\},
\]
with \(M_N=0\) if the set is empty.  On \(M_N\ge2\), define
\(\bar B_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}B_{\ell,N}\) and
\[
\widehat\Sigma_N^{Y}
=
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)
(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top .
\]
Then \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one and, with the harmless
auxiliary definition \(\widehat\Sigma_N^{Y}=0_{d\times d}\) on \(M_N<2\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-rate-main}
\left\|\widehat\Sigma_N^{Y}-\frac{\mathcal T_m}{\mu}
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\end{equation}

Moreover, let \(\Delta_N=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) be an \(\RR^d\)-valued
finite-record plug-in error and set
\[
\widehat Y_j=Y_j-\Delta_N L_j,\qquad
\widehat B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}\widehat Y_j,\qquad
\overline{\widehat B}_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\widehat B_{\ell,N}.
\]
The mean \(\overline{\widehat B}_N\) is used only on \(M_N\ge2\).
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:calendar-stopped-plugin-block-main}
\max_{1\le\ell\le M_N}
\left\|\widehat B_{\ell,N}-B_{\ell,N}\right\|
=O_{\PP}(b_NN^{-1/2})
\end{equation}
and the Algorithm~1 covariance object with plug-in rewards satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:calendar-stopped-plugin-covariance-rate-main}
\left\|
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top
-\frac{\mathcal T_m}{\mu}
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right),
\end{equation}
again with the auxiliary zero completion on \(M_N<2\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The renewal estimates
\[
K_N=N/\mu+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N),\qquad
\max_{j\le K_N+m+q+1}L_j=O_{\PP}(\log N)
\]
follow from the exponential moment.  Hence the fixed endpoint/look-ahead
deletion changes the stopped batch count by at most \(O(1)\), and
\[
M_N=\frac{N}{\mu(b_N+m)}
     +O_{\PP}\!\left(\frac{\sqrt N}{b_N}+1\right).
\]
Conditional on any deterministic prefix in the high-probability range
\(\ell\le C N/b_N\), the separated batch sums are independent by the
range-\(m\) separator.  The deterministic-prefix estimate in
\Cref{thm:regenerative-separated-batch-covariance-main}, combined with the
random-prefix replacement above and \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\), gives
\eqref{eq:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-rate-main}.  For the plug-in part,
\[
\widehat B_{\ell,N}-B_{\ell,N}
=-\Delta_N\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j,
\]
and a Chernoff bound plus a union bound over \(O_{\PP}(N/b_N)\) retained
batches gives the uniform block bound
\eqref{eq:calendar-stopped-plugin-block-main}.  Expanding the centered
quadratic covariance then gives the same cross-term and pure plug-in bounds as
in the main adjacent-gap theorem, proving
\eqref{eq:calendar-stopped-plugin-covariance-rate-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Standard complete-cycle and separated-batch facts]\label{prop:standard-complete-cycle-separated-batch-facts-main}
Let \((A_n)_{n\in\mathbb Z}\) be a stationary binary renewal process with
click epochs \((\tau_j)\), i.i.d. gaps \(G_j\), cycle lengths
\(L_j=G_j+1\), mean \(\mu=\EE L_0\in(0,\infty)\), and
\(\EE e^{\eta G_0}<\infty\) for some \(\eta>0\).  Let
\(K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}\), with \(K_N=-1\) if no click is observed.  The
following standard regenerative conclusions are used in the main text
\cite{GlynnIglehart1990STS,AsmussenGlynn2007,GlynnWhitt1991BatchMeans,
VatsFlegalJones2019Multivariate}.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},leftmargin=2.4em,itemsep=0.25em]
\item Every bounded local window
\[
Z_n=\psi(A_{n-r},\ldots,A_{n+\ell}),\qquad \bar z=\EE Z_0,
\]
has a complete-cycle reward
\[
U_j=\sum_{n=\tau_j}^{\tau_{j+1}-1}Z_n,\qquad
Y_j=U_j-\bar zL_j,
\]
with Palm centering
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-window-palm-inversion-main}
\EE U_j=\mu\bar z,\qquad \EE Y_j=0 .
\end{equation}
Moreover \(Y_j\) is a measurable function of finitely many adjacent gaps and
is therefore fixed-range dependent.  Endpoint and fixed look-ahead/look-back
trimming changes the calendar sum by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:local-window-endpoint-main}
\sum_{n=0}^{N-1}Z_n-\sum_j U_j=O_{\PP}(\log N),
\end{equation}
where the cycle sum is over the complete cycles intersecting the observed
calendar window, with the same \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\) bound after any fixed
endpoint trimming.

\item If \(Y_j=f(G_j,\ldots,G_{j+m_{\rm dep}})\in\RR^{d_{\rm rew}}\),
\(\EE Y_j=0\), and
\[
\|f(g_0,\ldots,g_{m_{\rm dep}})\|
\le C_f\left(1+\sum_{i=0}^{m_{\rm dep}}g_i\right),
\]
then, for any all-complete-cycle index set \(J_N\) obtained from
\(\{0,\ldots,K_N-1\}\) by deleting only fixed endpoint/look-ahead indices,
\[
\sqrt N\,\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_N}Y_j
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N\left(0,\frac{\mathcal T_{m_{\rm dep}}}{\mu}\right),
\]
where
\[
\mathcal T_{m_{\rm dep}}
=\Var(Y_0)+
\sum_{h=1}^{m_{\rm dep}}
\{\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_h)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_h,Y_0)\}.
\]

\item For \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\),
\(d_N=b_N+m_{\rm dep}\), and separated batches
\[
I_{\ell,N}:=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\},
\qquad
C_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j=(\ell-1)d_N}^{(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1}Y_j ,
\]
let \(M_N\) be the number of all-complete-cycle batches retained before
calendar time \(N\), and put
\(\bar C_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}C_{\ell,N}\) on \(M_N\ge2\).  Then the
retained batch covariance satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:separated-batch-covariance-estimator-rate-main}
\left\|
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}(C_{\ell,N}-\bar C_N)(C_{\ell,N}-\bar C_N)^\top
-\frac{\mathcal T_{m_{\rm dep}}}{\mu}
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{b_N/N}\right).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, before multiplying by \(K_N/N\), the cycle-time sample covariance
estimates \(\mathcal T_{m_{\rm dep}}\) at the same rate.

\item If plug-in rewards have the form
\[
\widehat Y_j=Y_j-(\widehat r_N-r)L_j,\qquad
\widehat r_N-r=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}),
\]
and \(b_N\) is polynomial, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:separated-batch-plugin-block-main}
\max_{\ell\le M_N}
\left\|\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}(\widehat Y_j-Y_j)\right\|
=O_{\PP}(b_NN^{-1/2})
\end{equation}
and replacing \(Y_j\) by \(\widehat Y_j\) in the separated-batch covariance
changes it by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:separated-batch-plugin-covariance-main}
O_{\PP}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right)
+O_{\PP}\!\left(\frac{b_N}{N}\right).
\end{equation}

\item For every fixed \(C\), \(\PP(K_N<C)\to0\), and the same conclusion holds
after deleting any fixed number of endpoint, look-back, or look-ahead cycles.

\item If \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\), and
\(M_N=O_{\PP}(N/b_N)\), then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:uniform-calendar-block-length-main}
\max_{1\le \ell\le M_N}
\frac1{b_N}\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j
=O_{\PP}(1).
\end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The only model-specific input to this proposition is that the click epochs
form a stationary renewal process with an exponential gap moment.  The
Palm-cycle identity gives
\[
\EE Z_0=\frac{\EE U_j}{\EE L_j},\qquad
U_j=\sum_{n=\tau_j}^{\tau_{j+1}-1}Z_n,
\]
and hence \(\EE U_j=\mu\bar z\) and \(\EE Y_j=0\).  Because \(Z_n\) is a fixed
local window, \(Y_j\) depends on only finitely many adjacent gaps; call this
range \(m_{\rm dep}\).  Exponential tails imply that the largest cycle
intersecting any fixed enlargement of \([0,N-1]\) is \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\), so
endpoint, look-ahead, and look-back deletions give
\eqref{eq:local-window-endpoint-main}.

The CLT and the calendar-time scale are the standard regenerative-reward
facts: apply the ordinary finite-range-dependent CLT to the stationary
sequence \(Y_j=f(G_j,\ldots,G_{j+m_{\rm dep}})\), then use the renewal law of
large numbers \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\) and the usual random-index replacement
\cite[Chs.~IV.3 and VI.3]{AsmussenGlynn2007}.  This gives the covariance
\(\mathcal T_{m_{\rm dep}}/\mu\).

For the separated-batch estimator, apply the fixed-range wrapper
\Cref{prop:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin-main} to the gap reward
\(Y_j=f(G_j,\ldots,G_{j+m_{\rm dep}})\), with \(q\) fixed endpoint and
look-ahead cycles deleted by the particular complete-cycle set.  It gives the
random calendar-stopped batch count bound
\(M_N=K_N/(b_N+m_{\rm dep})+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N/b_N+1)\), controls the maximal
deleted endpoint cycle, subtracts the empirical batch mean, and gives exactly
\eqref{eq:separated-batch-covariance-estimator-rate-main}.

For the plug-in perturbation, write
\(\Delta_N=\widehat r_N-r=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Each batch changes by
\[
\widehat C_{\ell,N}-C_{\ell,N}
=-\Delta_N\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}L_j .
\]
\Cref{prop:calendar-stopped-separated-batch-plugin-main} gives the uniform
block-length bound, the block perturbation
\eqref{eq:separated-batch-plugin-block-main}, and the covariance perturbation
\eqref{eq:separated-batch-plugin-covariance-main}.  Finally,
\((K_N+1)/N\to1/\mu\) gives the fixed-low-cycle statement, and the preceding
uniform block-length bound is exactly \eqref{eq:uniform-calendar-block-length-main}.
\end{proof}



 succeeded in 176ms:
\section{Killed-reset fibres: an exact two-state arc and higher-state structure}

The minimal-realization statements in this subsection are recalled background,
not contributions of this paper.  Kalman's similarity theorem is the classical
source, and minimal MAP/phase-type formulations and canonical-coordinate
developments are given in
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems,TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP,
BladtNielsen2017MatrixExponential,MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical,
PappTelek2013DiscretePHCanonical}.  We state the consequence needed to locate
the visible fibre, classify the complete Markovian fibre of the physical
two-state kernel, and then prove the higher-state quantitative assertion: at a
minimal interior killed-reset kernel the admissible fibre has local dimension
\((n-1)^2\).  Nonunique MAP and Coxian representatives are documented in
\cite{RamirezCoboLilloWiper2010MAP2Nonidentifiability,
RamirezCoboLillo2012WeakEquivalentMAP,RizkBurkeWalsh2019CoxianNonuniqueness}.
Graph-specific time-to-event inverses are treated algebraically by
Radulescu et al.\ \cite{RadulescuEtAl2025TimeToEventIdentifiability}; the
criterion below instead isolates the exact orbit fibre for any declared
minimal killed-reset subclass.

\paragraph{Recalled minimal-realization uniqueness.}
Let \((a,A,b)\) and \((\widetilde a,\widetilde A,\widetilde b)\) be two real
scalar realizations of the same dimension \(n\) such that
\[
aA^k b=\widetilde a\widetilde A^k\widetilde b
\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Assume that both reachability matrices
\[
[\,b,Ab,\ldots,A^{n-1}b\,],\qquad
[\,\widetilde b,\widetilde A\widetilde b,\ldots,
\widetilde A^{n-1}\widetilde b\,]
\]
and both observability matrices
\[
\begin{bmatrix}a\\aA\\\vdots\\aA^{n-1}\end{bmatrix},
\qquad
\begin{bmatrix}\widetilde a\\\widetilde a\widetilde A\\\vdots\\
\widetilde a\widetilde A^{n-1}\end{bmatrix}
\]
have rank \(n\). Then there is a unique \(M\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\RR)\)
such that
\[
\widetilde a=aM,
\qquad \widetilde A=M^{-1}AM,
\qquad \widetilde b=M^{-1}b.
\]
These rank hypotheses are precisely properties \textup{(R)} and \textup{(L)}
of Bladt and Nielsen, whose Theorem~4.2.16 characterizes minimality by their
simultaneous validity; the corresponding intertwining relations are given in
their Lemmas~4.2.5--4.2.6
\cite[Theorem~4.2.16, p.~216, and Lemmas~4.2.5--4.2.6,
p.~212]{BladtNielsen2017MatrixExponential}. This standard result is invoked
here and is not claimed as a new theorem; see also Kalman
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems}, Telek--Horv\'ath
\cite{TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP}, and
\cite{Ryden1996Aggregated,OCinneide1989PhaseTypeNonuniqueness,
OCinneide1991InvariantPolytopes}.

\begin{proposition}[Recalled orbit-fibre consequence of minimal realization]
\label{thm:nstate-killed-reset-identifiability-dichotomy-main}
Fix \(n\ge2\), put \(\beta=e_n^\top\), and let
\[
\mathcal K_n=\{K\in\RR^{n\times n}:K\ge0,\ K\mathbf1\le\mathbf1,\
\operatorname{spr}(K)<1\}.
\]
For \(K\in\mathcal K_n\), define the killed-reset D-MAP by
\[
T_0=K,\qquad T_1=(I-K)\mathbf1\beta
\]
and its Palm survival coordinates by \(S_k(K)=\beta K^k\mathbf1\).
Call \(K\) minimal when both
\[
\mathcal C(K)=[\,\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1\,],
\qquad
\mathcal O(K)=
\begin{bmatrix}\beta\\ \beta K\\ \vdots\\ \beta K^{n-1}\end{bmatrix}
\]
have rank \(n\). Let
\[
\mathcal G_n=\{M\in\operatorname{GL}_n(\RR):
M\mathbf1=\mathbf1,\ \beta M=\beta\},
\]
and let \(\mathfrak P_n\subset\mathcal G_n\) be the reset-preserving
permutation matrices.

For any declared subclass \(\mathcal C\subset\mathcal K_n\) of minimal
kernels, fix the structural equivalence \(\sim_{\mathcal C}\) under which
identification is requested, and assume that \(K\sim_{\mathcal C}L\) implies
equality of visible laws. Examples are reset-preserving hidden-state
relabeling and, in a serial rate chart, permutation of the rate coordinates.
Define the Markovian orbit fibre
\[
\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)=
\left\{M^{-1}KM\in\mathcal C:M\in\mathcal G_n\right\}
\big/\!\sim_{\mathcal C}.
\]
Then standard minimal-realization uniqueness gives the following exact
characterization.
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
\item Two minimal kernels \(K,L\in\mathcal K_n\) generate the same stationary
visible click law if and only if
\[
\begin{aligned}
L&=M^{-1}KM &&\text{for the unique }M\in\mathcal G_n\\
&&&\text{implementing their normalized minimal realizations}.
\end{aligned}
\]
\item Equivalently, the declared hidden object is identifiable from the visible law within
\(\mathcal C\), modulo \(\sim_{\mathcal C}\), if and only if
\[
\#\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)=1\qquad\text{for every }K\in\mathcal C.
\]
Pointwise identifiability at \(K\) is equivalent to the same condition at
that \(K\); if the cardinality exceeds one, its distinct classes are exactly
the hidden nonidentifiability fibre. Thus this assertion is the orbit-fibre
reformulation of part \textup{(i)}.
\end{enumerate}
Part~\textup{(ii)} is deliberately recorded only as terminology: after the
fibre has been defined, singleton fibre and identifiability modulo
\(\sim_{\mathcal C}\) are the same assertion.  Neither part is claimed as a
new realization theorem.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Deterministic reset makes the visible process renewal. Its Palm gap tail is
exactly \(S_k(K)\), so equality of visible stationary laws is equivalent to
equality of all the scalar coordinates \(\beta K^k\mathbf1\), \(k\ge0\).
Apply the minimal-realization theorem stated above to the triples
\((\beta,K,\mathbf1)\) and \((\beta,L,\mathbf1)\). Its hypotheses are exactly
the two displayed rank conditions in the proposition, and it gives a unique
similarity \(L=M^{-1}KM\). Preservation of the fixed input and output vectors
gives \(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\) and \(\beta M=\beta\), respectively.
Conversely, these two identities give
\[
\beta(M^{-1}KM)^k\mathbf1=\beta K^k\mathbf1
\]
for every \(k\), proving (i). Taking the intersection with
\(\mathcal C\) and then quotienting precisely the declared structural
equivalence proves
the necessary-and-sufficient assertion (ii).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Complete two-state fibre arc and exchange dichotomy]
\label{thm:two-state-complete-fibre-arc-main}
Under \Cref{ass:model-inference-contract-main}, put \(K=T_0\),
\(q_\ast=\Gamma/\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), and
\[
 I_\ast=[\min\{1,q_\ast\},\max\{1,q_\ast\}].
\]
Then \(K\) is minimal, and its complete visible-law fibre in
\(\mathcal K_2\) is the injectively parametrized closed arc
\[
 \{K(q):q\in I_\ast\},
 \qquad
 K(q)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 p-b+bq & \displaystyle\frac{b(1-q)(q-q_\ast)}q\\[5pt]
 bq & s+b(1-q)
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{F1}
\]
Here complete means that every \(L\in\mathcal K_2\) with the same stationary
visible click law as \(K\) is \(K(q)\) for exactly one \(q\in I_\ast\).
Writing \(T_0(\alpha,\eta)\) for the no-click kernel constructed with attempt
rate \(\alpha\) and recovery rate \(\eta\), the endpoints are the two physical
triangular kernels obtained by exchanging those rates:
\[
 K(1)=T_0(\Gamma,\kappa_{\mathrm r}),
 \qquad
 K(q_\ast)=T_0(\kappa_{\mathrm r},\Gamma).
 \tag{F2}
\]
If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), every \(q\) strictly between the
endpoints gives a strictly positive kernel with strictly positive killing
deficits, and the physical triangular subfamily meets the arc only at the two
endpoints.  If \(\Gamma=\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), then \(I_\ast=\{1\}\), so
the entire two-state killed-reset fibre is a singleton.  Thus the unrestricted
two-state hidden kernel is identifiable exactly on the exchange diagonal and
has a continuum fibre off it; within the physical sampled-counter subfamily,
the only ambiguity off the diagonal is the rate exchange.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
First \(b>0\).  If \(\Gamma\ne\kappa_{\mathrm r}\), direct evaluation of
the defining integral gives
\[
 b=\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(p-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma},
 \qquad
 b+s-p=\frac{\Gamma(p-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}
          =bq_\ast>0.
 \tag{F3}
\]
The same identities hold by continuity on the diagonal, where
\(p=s\), \(b=\Gamma\Delta\tau p>0\), and \(q_\ast=1\).  Consequently
\[
 \det[\mathbf1,K\mathbf1]=b+s-p=bq_\ast>0,
 \qquad
 \det\begin{bmatrix}e_2^\top\\e_2^\top K\end{bmatrix}=-b\ne0,
\]
so \(K\) is minimal.

Every member of \(\mathcal G_2\) has the unique form
\[
 M_q=\begin{pmatrix}q&1-q\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad q\ne0.
\]
Multiplication, using \(b+s-p=bq_\ast\), gives
\(M_q^{-1}KM_q=K(q)\) in \textup{(F1)}.  Since \(K\) is minimal, its scalar
Hankel sequence has rank two.  Any \(L\in\mathcal K_2\) with the same visible
law has the same leading Hankel matrix; its factorization as
\(\mathcal O(L)\mathcal C(L)\) has rank two, so both factors have rank two and
\(L\) is minimal as well.  The recalled orbit--fibre proposition therefore
forces every such \(L\) to have the displayed form.  If
\(K(q)\in\mathcal K_2\), its lower-left entry
is \(bq\ge0\).  Invertibility excludes \(q=0\), hence \(q>0\).  Its
upper-right entry then yields the exact condition
\[
 \frac{b(1-q)(q-q_\ast)}q\ge0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad q\in I_\ast.
 \tag{F4}
\]

Conversely, let \(q\in I_\ast\).  The two diagonal entries in
\textup{(F1)} are affine in \(q\), with endpoint values \(p,s\) and \(s,p\),
respectively, and are therefore positive.  The lower-left entry is positive,
and \textup{(F4)} makes the upper-right entry nonnegative.  Writing
\(a=1-b-s>0\), the two killing deficits are
\[
 \mathbf1-K(q)\mathbf1
 =\begin{pmatrix}a+bq_\ast/q\\a\end{pmatrix}>0.
 \tag{F5}
\]
Similarity preserves the spectral radius
\(\max\{p,s\}<1\), so \(K(q)\in\mathcal K_2\).  This proves that the
displayed arc is the complete fibre.  It is injective because its lower-left
entry is \(bq\).

At \(q=1\) one recovers \(K\).  At \(q=q_\ast\),
\[
 K(q_\ast)=\begin{pmatrix}s&0\\bq_\ast&p\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Equation~\textup{(F3)} says that \(bq_\ast\) is precisely the no-click
recovery-to-ready probability after exchanging the two rates, and its second
row has the same killing deficit \(a\); this proves \textup{(F2)}.  If
\(q_\ast\ne1\), the product in the upper-right entry is strictly positive
inside \(I_\ast\) and vanishes only at its endpoints.  All remaining entries
and both deficits are already strict there.  If \(q_\ast=1\),
\textup{(F4)} forces \(q=1\).  The endpoint, triangular-intersection, and
identifiability statements follow.
\end{proof}

The generalized-Erlang representation and its Coxian minimality theory are due
to He--Zhang \cite{HeZhang2009GeneralizedErlang,HeZhang2008MinimalCoxian}.
The following corollary is their serial phase-type structure specialized to
finitely many sampled Palm-tail coordinates; the confluent calculation records
how repeated rates remain recoverable in that specialization.

\begin{corollary}[Finite-coordinate serial specialization with collisions]
\label{cor:nstate-serial-all-collision-identifiability-main}
Fix \(n\ge2\), a known \(\Delta\tau>0\), and arbitrary positive rates
\(\theta=(\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n)\), with repetitions allowed. Let
\[
Q(\theta)_{ii}=-\theta_i,\qquad
Q(\theta)_{i,i-1}=\theta_i\quad(2\le i\le n),
\]
with all other entries zero, and put
\[
T_0(\theta)=e^{\Delta\tau Q(\theta)},\qquad
T_1(\theta)=(I-T_0(\theta))\mathbf1e_n^\top.
\]
Then the visible record identifies exactly the unordered rate multiset
\(\{\!\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_n\}\!\}\). Equivalently, the fibre of the
labelled serial parameter is its \(\mathfrak S_n\)-permutation orbit.

More explicitly, if the distinct rates are
\(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r\) with multiplicities
\(m_1,\ldots,m_r\), and \(z_j=e^{-\lambda_j\Delta\tau}\), then the Palm tail
has the confluent Prony form
\[
S_k=\sum_{j=1}^r\sum_{\ell=0}^{m_j-1}
c_{j\ell}k^\ell z_j^k,\qquad c_{j,m_j-1}\ne0,
\]
and its minimal recurrence polynomial is
\[
\chi_\theta(x)=\prod_{j=1}^r(x-z_j)^{m_j}.
\]
The leading \(n\times n\) Hankel matrix
\((S_{i+j})_{0\le i,j<n}\) is invertible; hence
\(S_0,\ldots,S_{2n-1}\) determine \(\chi_\theta\), including all collision
multiplicities. Pole collision therefore changes conditioning and the local
root chart, but does not change population identifiability.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Starting in phase \(n\), the absorption time is
\(W=X_n+\cdots+X_1\), where the \(X_i\) are independent exponentials with
rates \(\theta_i\). Its Laplace transform is
\[
\EE e^{-sW}=\prod_{i=1}^n\frac{\theta_i}{s+\theta_i}.
\]
Consequently
\[
\int_0^\infty e^{-st}\PP(W>t)\,dt
=\frac{1-\prod_{i=1}^n\theta_i/(s+\theta_i)}{s}
\]
has at
\(-\lambda_j\) a pole of exact order \(m_j\). Partial-fraction inversion and
sampling at \(t=k\Delta\tau\) give the displayed exponential-polynomial form
with nonzero top confluent coefficient. Standard confluent Prony algebra
then gives the exact minimal annihilator \(\chi_\theta\)
\cite{KunisRoemerVonDerOhe2020Prony}.

For completeness of the finite-coordinate assertion, the matrices
\(\mathcal O(Q)\) and \(\mathcal C(Q)\) are nonsingular: successive rows
\(e_n^\top Q^j\) and successive columns \(Q^j\mathbf1\) expose the nonzero
bidiagonal products triangularly. Because the exponential is injective on
the real spectrum of \(Q\), Hermite interpolation supplies a polynomial
\(p\) with \(Q=p(e^{\Delta\tau Q})\); hence the corresponding observation and
reachability spaces for \(T_0=e^{\Delta\tau Q}\) are also full. Therefore
\[
(S_{i+j})_{0\le i,j<n}=\mathcal O(T_0)\mathcal C(T_0)
\]
is invertible, and the first \(2n\) tails solve uniquely for the order-\(n\)
recurrence. The roots and their algebraic multiplicities recover
\(\lambda_j=-\Delta\tau^{-1}\log z_j\) and \(m_j\). Conversely, permuting
the independent summands does not change \(W\), the Palm gap law, or the
stationary renewal law. This proves the exact fibre claim.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Quantitative interior fibre dimension and positivity persistence]
\label{thm:nstate-interior-continuum-nonidentifiability-main}
For the unrestricted class with \(\sim_{\mathcal K_n}\) equal to
reset-preserving state relabelling, the hidden kernel is locally
nonidentifiable at every minimal interior point \(K\), where interior means
\(K>0\) and \((I-K)\mathbf1>0\) entrywise. More precisely, the
reset-preserving similarity group has Lie algebra
\[
\mathfrak g_n=\{B:B\mathbf1=0,\ \beta B=0\},
\qquad \dim\mathfrak g_n=(n-1)^2,
\]
and the orbit map
\[
\psi_K:\mathcal G_n\longrightarrow\mathbb R^{n\times n},\qquad
\psi_K(M)=M^{-1}KM,
\]
has injective differential
\[
D\psi_K(I)B=[K,B]\qquad(B\in\mathfrak g_n).
\]
Consequently the visible fibre through \(K\) contains, in a neighbourhood of
\(K\), a smooth immersed manifold of dimension exactly \((n-1)^2\).  Every
kernel on a sufficiently small neighbourhood of this orbit manifold remains
strictly substochastic: its entries and killing deficits are positive.  After
quotienting by the finite reset-preserving permutation group, the local fibre
still has dimension \((n-1)^2\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Because \(\beta\mathbf1=1\),
\(\RR^n=\operatorname{span}\{\mathbf1\}\oplus\ker\beta\).  Relative to this
decomposition, \(B\mathbf1=0\) removes the first block column and
\(\beta B=0\) forces the range into \(\ker\beta\).  Thus
\(B=\operatorname{diag}(0,A)\), with arbitrary
\(A\in\operatorname{End}(\ker\beta)\), and
\(\dim\mathfrak g_n=(n-1)^2\).

Differentiating \(\exp(-tB)K\exp(tB)\) at zero gives
\(D\psi_K(I)B=[K,B]\).  Suppose \([K,B]=0\).  Since \(B\mathbf1=0\),
\[
BK^j\mathbf1=K^jB\mathbf1=0,
\qquad j=0,\ldots,n-1.
\]
The reachability matrix
\([\mathbf1,K\mathbf1,\ldots,K^{n-1}\mathbf1]\) has rank \(n\), so these
equalities imply \(B=0\).  Hence the differential is injective on all of
\(\mathfrak g_n\), not merely in one selected direction.

The constant-rank theorem now makes the image of \(\psi_K\) near the identity
a smooth immersed manifold of dimension \((n-1)^2\).  For every
\(M\in\mathcal G_n\),
\[
\beta\psi_K(M)^j\mathbf1=\beta K^j\mathbf1\qquad(j\ge0),
\]
so the recalled orbit-fibre result places this entire manifold in the visible
fibre.  Finally, all entries of \(M^{-1}KM\) and all components of
\((I-M^{-1}KM)\mathbf1\) depend continuously on \(M\).  Their values at
\(M=I\) are strictly positive; a sufficiently small identity neighbourhood
therefore remains inside the interior Markovian cone.  The stabilizer has zero
Lie algebra by injectivity of the differential, and quotienting by the finite
reset-preserving permutation group cannot change local dimension.  This proves
all assertions.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Nonminimal and graph-specific open interfaces]
\label{rem:nstate-identifiability-open-interfaces-main}
The characterization and the two headline results deliberately assume
Hankel/minimal rank \(n\). If a nominal
\(n\)-state representation has lower visible rank, equal laws need not be
related by an invertible \(n\times n\) similarity; a sharp classification then
requires a stratified treatment of nonminimal augmentations. For a fixed
nonserial zero pattern on the boundary of the Markovian cone, evaluating the
orbit intersection \(\mathfrak F_{\mathcal C}(K)\) is an exact semialgebraic
problem, but no graph-uniform closed form is claimed here. Finally, collision
multiplicity is population-identifiable in the serial model but statistically
singular under noisy finite records. These are explicit interfaces, not
consequences of the present results.
\end{remark}

For orientation, the unrestricted fibre is already nontrivial in two states:
\[
K=\begin{pmatrix}1/2&0\\1/2&1/4\end{pmatrix},\qquad
M=\begin{pmatrix}9/10&1/10\\0&1\end{pmatrix},\qquad
M^{-1}KM=\begin{pmatrix}9/20&1/45\\9/20&3/10\end{pmatrix}.
\]
Both kernels are nonnegative and substochastic,
\(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\), \(e_2^\top M=e_2^\top\), and their entire visible
renewal laws coincide, although the hidden kernels do not.
