 506: 
 507: When $K_N\ge3$, put
 508: 
 509: \[
 510: \widehat\nu_N=\frac1{K_N}\sum_{j=1}^{K_N}
 511: h_{\widehat\theta_N}(G_j)^2,
 512: \qquad
 513: \widehat I_N=\frac{K_N}{N}\widehat\nu_N^2,
 514: \]
 515: 
 516: \[
 517: Z_N=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N\widehat I_N}}
 518: \sum_{j=1}^{K_N-1}
 519: h_{\widehat\theta_N}(G_j)h_{\widehat\theta_N}(G_{j+1}).
 520: \]
 521: 
 522: If $K_N<3$ or $\widehat I_N=0$, set $Z_N=0$.  Thus, for every binary
 523: record of length $N\ge3$, the test
 524: 
 525: \[
 526: \varphi_N=\mathbf1_{\{d_N>N^{-1/4}\ \text{or}\ Z_N>z_{1-\alpha}\}}
 527: \tag{F}
 528: \]
 529: 
 530: is defined without empirical roots, logarithms, or evaluations of the analytic
 531: root chart outside its domain.  It has the following properties.
 532: 
 533: \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
 534: \item Its null size is compact-uniform:
 535: \[
 536: \sup_{\theta\in\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}}
 537: \left|\PP_\theta(\varphi_N=1)-\alpha\right|\longrightarrow0.
 538: \]
 539: This includes the exchange diagonal and every null triangular array, in
 540: particular arrays satisfying $N^{1/4}|p_N-s_N|=O(1)$.
 541: 
 542: \item If $P$ is a fixed stationary ergodic binary law and
 543: $d(P,\mathcal P_0)>0$, then
 544: \[
 545: \PP_P(\varphi_N=1)\longrightarrow1.
 546: \]
 547: The convergence is uniform on any class with a common geometric
 548: $\alpha$-mixing envelope $\alpha_P(k)\le Cq^k$, $q<1$, and a common
 549: separation $d(P,\mathcal P_0)\ge\delta>0$.  No consistency assertion is made
 550: for arbitrary stationary nonergodic mixtures.
 551: 
 552: \item Fix an interior exchange point $\theta_0=(x_0,x_0)$, and abbreviate
 553: $g_k=g_{\theta_0}(k)$, $\mu=\sum_{k\ge0}(k+1)g_k$, and
 554: \[
 555: h(k)=\mathbf1_{\{k=1\}}-\frac{g_1}{g_2}\mathbf1_{\{k=2\}}.
 556: \]
 557: For $0\le\eta<g_2/g_1$, the transition matrix
 558: \[
 559: Q_\eta(\ell\mid k)=g_\ell\{1+\eta h(k)h(\ell)\}
 560: \tag{G}
 561: \]
 562: is stochastic, has stationary marginal $g$, and defines by Palm inversion a
 563: stationary nonrenewal visible law when $\eta>0$.  This law preserves exactly
 564: the sampled-counter coordinates $r_0,r_1,r_2$, because
 565: \[
 566: r_0=\mu^{-1},\qquad r_1=\mu^{-1}g_0,
 567: \qquad r_2=\mu^{-1}\{g_1+g_0Q_\eta(0\mid0)\},
 568: \]
 569: and $Q_\eta(0\mid0)=g_0$.  Nevertheless,
 570: \[
 571: \PP_\eta(10101)=\mu^{-1}g_1^2(1+\eta),
 572: \]
 573: so a length-five word separates every $\eta>0$ from the renewal null.
 574: 
 575: \item Put
 576: \[
 577: \nu=\EE_g h(G)^2=g_1+\frac{g_1^2}{g_2},
 578: \qquad \mathcal I=\frac{\nu^2}{\mu}.
 579: \]
 580: Along $\eta_N=t/\sqrt N$, $t\ge0$, the stationary record experiments in
 581: part~\textup{(iii)} satisfy
 582: \[
 583: \log\frac{dP_{\eta_N}^{(N)}}{dP_0^{(N)}}
 584: =t\Delta_N-\frac12t^2\mathcal I+o_{P_0}(1),
 585: \qquad \Delta_N\Rightarrow N(0,\mathcal I).
 586: \]
 587: Moreover, $\widehat I_N\to_P\mathcal I$ and
 588: \[
 589: Z_N\Rightarrow N(t\sqrt{\mathcal I},1),
 590: \qquad
 591: \pi_\alpha(t)=1-\Phi_{\rm N}
 592: \{z_{1-\alpha}-t\sqrt{\mathcal I}\}.
 593: \]
 594: Thus $\pi_\alpha(t)>\alpha$ for every $t>0$.  If
 595: $\eta_N=o(N^{-1/2})$, the Kullback--Leibler divergence tends to zero, while
 596: the score component of \textup{(F)} attains the Neyman--Pearson power envelope
 597: of this one-dimensional Gaussian-shift subexperiment at the $N^{-1/2}$
 598: rate.
 599: \end{enumerate}
 600: 
 601: The distributional-distance guard in parts~\textup{(i)}--\textup{(ii)} is a
 602: specialization of established stationary-ergodic testing methods
 603: \cite{RyabkoRyabko2010ErgodicInference,Ryabko2012StationaryTesting}.  Generic
 604: LAN and Gaussian-shift optimality are also standard
 605: \cite{vanDerVaart1998AsymptoticStatistics}.  The model-specific assertions are
 606: the exchange-uniform finite-range score, the exactly
 607: $(r_0,r_1,r_2)$-preserving alternative \textup{(G)}, and their joint use with
 608: the complete visible-law guard.
 609: \end{paperthm}
 610: 
 611: \begin{proof}
 612: On $\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}$, the hidden transition matrix is
 613: \[
 614: T_0+T_1=\begin{pmatrix}p&1-p\\b&1-b\end{pmatrix}.
 615: \]
 616: Each entry has a positive parameter-independent lower bound.  The hidden chain
 617: therefore satisfies a uniform Doeblin condition, and every bounded function of
 618: the visible record has a common geometric mixing envelope.  For a word $w$
 619: of length $m\le N$, overlap for lags below $m$ and the mixing covariance
 620: bound above that lag give
 621: \[
 622: \sup_\theta\operatorname{Var}_\theta\{\widehat P_N[w]\}
 623: \le \frac{Cm}{N}.
 624: \]
 625: Consequently, by Cauchy--Schwarz,
 626: \[
 627: \sup_\theta\EE_\theta
 628: \sum_{m=1}^{L_N}4^{-m}\sum_w
 629: |\widehat P_N[w]-P_\theta[w]|
 630: \le \frac{C'}{\sqrt N}
 631: \sum_{m\ge1}2^{-m}\sqrt m=O(N^{-1/2}).
 632: \]
 633: Since the minimum defining $d_N$ is no larger than its value at the true
 634: parameter, Markov's inequality yields
 635: \[
 636: \sup_\theta\PP_\theta(d_N>N^{-1/4})=O(N^{-1/4}).
 637: \tag{1}
 638: \]
 639: 
 640: Let $\widehat\sigma_N=(\widehat p_N+\widehat s_N,
 641: \widehat p_N\widehat s_N)$ and $\sigma_\theta=(p+s,ps)$.  Define the empirical
 642: inclusion vector
 643: \[
 644: \widehat r_N^{\rm word}
 645: =\{\widehat P_N[1],\widehat P_N[11],
 646: \widehat P_N[101]+\widehat P_N[111]\}^\top.
 647: \]
 648: For every model parameter $\vartheta$, the corresponding coordinates obey
 649: $r_0=P_\vartheta[1]$, $r_1=P_\vartheta[11]$, and
 650: $r_2=P_\vartheta[101]+P_\vartheta[111]$.  Since all these words have length at
 651: most three, the definition of the truncated objective gives the deterministic
 652: bound
 653: \[
 654: \|\widehat r_N^{\rm word}-r(\widehat\theta_N)\|
 655: \le C_0d_N.
 656: \]
 657: Consequently, for the true parameter $\theta$,
 658: \[
 659: \|r(\widehat\theta_N)-r(\theta)\|
 660: \le C_0d_N+C_1\sum_{m\le3}\sum_{|w|=m}
 661: |\widehat P_N[w]-P_\theta[w]|.
 662: \tag{2a}
 663: \]
 664: The first part of the proof gives $d_N=O_{P_\theta}(N^{-1/2})$ uniformly,
 665: and the finite sum in \textup{(2a)} has the same uniform rate.  The uniformly
 666: bounded derivative of $\Phi$ on $\Theta_{\varepsilon,M}$ therefore gives
 667: \[
 668: \widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_\theta=O_{P_\theta}(N^{-1/2})
 669: \tag{2}
 670: \]
 671: uniformly.  No ordered-root map is used in this step.  For every fixed gap
 672: index, $g_\theta(k)$ is a symmetric analytic function of the two roots;
 673: compactness and $\inf_\theta g_\theta(2)>0$ make $h_\theta$ and its first
 674: two $\sigma$-derivatives uniformly bounded.
 675: 
 676: Under the null, the complete Palm gaps are consecutive members of an i.i.d.
 677: gap sequence.  With
 678: \[
 679: q_j(\theta)=h_\theta(G_j)h_\theta(G_{j+1}),
 680: \]
 681: one has
 682: \[
 683: \EE_\theta q_j=0,\qquad
 684: \operatorname{Var}_\theta(q_j)=\nu_\theta^2,
 685: \qquad
 686: \operatorname{Cov}_\theta(q_j,q_{j+1})=0,
 687: \]
 688: where $\nu_\theta=\EE_\theta h_\theta(G)^2$.  These rewards are bounded and
 689: one-dependent, $K_N/N\to1/\mu_\theta$, and the initial and terminal deletions
 690: affect only $o_P(\sqrt N)$ reward.  The uniform one-dependent CLT and the
 691: renewal-index replacement thus give
 692: \[
 693: \frac{\sum_{j=1}^{K_N-1}q_j(\theta)}
 694: {\sqrt{K_N}\nu_\theta}\Rightarrow N(0,1)
 695: \]
 696: uniformly in $\theta$.  The plug-in has no first-order effect: independence
 697: and $\EE_\theta h_\theta(G)=0$ imply
 698: \[
 699: \EE_\theta\left[
 700: \partial_\sigma\{h_\sigma(G_0)h_\sigma(G_1)\}
 701: \right]_{\sigma=\sigma_\theta}=0.
 702: \]
 703: Hence the sum of the first derivatives is $O_P(\sqrt N)$; multiplying by
 704: $(2)$, and bounding the summed Taylor remainder by
 705: $O_P(N\|\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_\theta\|^2)$, shows that both terms are
 706: $o_P(\sqrt N)$.  The same bounds give
 707: $\widehat I_N\to_P\nu_\theta^2/\mu_\theta$, uniformly.  Together with
 708: $(1)$, this proves part~\textup{(i)}.
 709: 
 710: For part~\textup{(ii)}, Birkhoff's theorem gives almost-sure convergence of
 711: every fixed empirical word probability under a stationary ergodic law.  For
 712: every $L$, the finitely many terms with $m\le L$ converge uniformly in
 713: $\theta$ by continuity and compactness, while, for all probability laws,
 714: \[
 715: \sum_{m>L}4^{-m}\sum_w|P[w]-Q[w]|\le2^{1-L}.
 716: \]
 717: It follows that $d_N\to d(P,\mathcal P_0)$ almost surely.  Under a common
 718: geometric mixing envelope the preceding variance calculation is uniform over
 719: the alternative class, which proves the stated uniform version.
 720: 
 721: For part~\textup{(iii)}, $\EE_g h=0$ gives both
 722: $\sum_\ell Q_\eta(\ell\mid k)=1$ and $gQ_\eta=g$.  The only negative
 723: product among nonzero score values is
 724: $h(1)h(2)=-g_1/g_2$; hence $0\le\eta<g_2/g_1$ gives a nonnegative kernel.
 725: Its rows depend on $k$ when $\eta>0$, so the stationary Palm gap sequence
 726: is not i.i.d.  The three inclusion identities follow by enumerating a click at
 727: lags zero, one, and two, and $h(0)=0$ gives
 728: $Q_\eta(0\mid0)=g_0$.  Two consecutive gaps equal to one produce the word
 729: $10101$, and
 730: \[
 731: \mu^{-1}g_1Q_\eta(1\mid1)
 732: =\mu^{-1}g_1^2(1+\eta),
 733: \]
 734: which proves the separation assertion.
 735: 
 736: Finally, write the observed click times as
 737: $0\le C_1<\cdots<C_J<N$, put $A=C_1$, $R=N-1-C_J$, and let
 738: $G_j=C_{j+1}-C_j-1$.  For $J\ge2$, Palm inversion gives the exact stationary
 739: record probability
 740: \[
 741: \frac1\mu\sum_{k\ge A}g_kQ_\eta(G_1\mid k)
 742: \prod_{j=1}^{J-2}Q_\eta(G_{j+1}\mid G_j)
 743: \sum_{\ell\ge R}Q_\eta(\ell\mid G_{J-1}).
 744: \tag{3}
 745: \]
 746: The factor $1/\mu$ is unchanged because the path preserves the invariant gap
 747: marginal $g$ and its mean.  If $S_a=\sum_{k\ge a}g_k$ and
 748: $\bar h_a=S_a^{-1}\sum_{k\ge a}g_kh(k)$, division of \textup{(3)} by its
 749: $\eta=0$ value gives
 750: \[
 751: \{1+\eta h(G_1)\bar h_A\}
 752: \prod_{j=1}^{J-2}\{1+\eta h(G_j)h(G_{j+1})\}
 753: \{1+\eta h(G_{J-1})\bar h_R\}.
 754: \tag{4}
 755: \]
 756: Here $|\bar h_a|\le\|h\|_\infty$, so for $\eta_N=t/\sqrt N$ the two endpoint
 757: log factors in \textup{(4)} are uniformly $O(N^{-1/2})=o(1)$.  The events
 758: $J<2$ have exponentially small null probability by the uniform gap-tail
 759: bound.  Thus the log likelihood ratio equals, up to $o_P(1)$,
 760: \[
 761: \sum_{j=1}^{J-2}\log\{1+\eta_Nh(G_j)h(G_{j+1})\}.
 762: \]
 763: The score is bounded.  Taylor expansion, the one-dependent CLT, and
 764: $K_N/N\to1/\mu$ give the displayed LAN expansion with
 765: $\mathcal I=\nu^2/\mu$.  The score is orthogonal to every renewal marginal
 766: score $s(G_j)$, since
 767: \[
 768: \EE\{h(G_j)h(G_{j+1})s(G_j)\}
 769: =\EE\{h(G_j)s(G_j)\}\EE h(G_{j+1})=0,
 770: \]
 771: and similarly at the other endpoint.  Thus estimating the symmetric renewal
 772: nuisance coordinates causes no information loss, including at the exchange
 773: boundary.  Under the local alternative, $d_N=O_P(N^{-1/2})$, so the guard in
 774: \textup{(F)} is inactive with probability tending to one.  Le Cam's third
 775: lemma gives the stated normal shift and power.  The local divergence is
 776: $N\eta_N^2\mathcal I/2+o(N\eta_N^2)$; contiguity below $N^{-1/2}$ and the
 777: Gaussian-shift Neyman--Pearson bound complete part~\textup{(iv)}.
 778: \end{proof}
 779:

"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw article_discussion.tex' in D:\omega\automath\papers\publication\2026_detector_shells_click_record_kms_jphyscomm

\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}

The deterministic marked reset turns the visible output of the constrained
D-MAP into a renewal process.  This is the source of both the explicit record
law and the low-dimensional quotient inverse.  It is also the source of the
model's fragility: a marked transition into the ready state removes the fixed
Palm restart and invalidates the renewal product used by the inverse and the
retained-cycle calculations.

The visible law determines the unordered survival pair, not the hidden labels.
The two-state fibre result makes that distinction concrete.  Away from the
exchange diagonal, weakly equivalent stochastic realizations in the
killed-reset cone form a closed arc with the two triangular rate orderings as
endpoints.  On the diagonal the arc collapses.  In higher state dimension the
\((n-1)^2\) orbit calculation applies to the minimal interior stratum and is
not a statement about the global dimension of every visible fibre.

The lettered inferential statements are deliberately downstream of the
population algebra.  Theorem~C estimates the three inclusion coordinates;
Theorem~D checks the complete visible law under its stated null and alternative
classes; Theorem~E transports Theorem~C through the stable quotient chart of
Theorem~B.  Sorted roots and log-rates are ordinary delta-method consequences.
The physical-image test is a separate finite-dimensional diagnostic.  The
abstract semiparametric, moving-dimensional coupling, complete-Palm-gap split,
and projection calculations are supplementary.

\section{Comparison with prior representation and tangent results}
\label{sec:theorem-led-comparison}

\Cref{tab:priority-comparison-main} states the comparison at the formula level.
The middle column identifies the specialization actually used; the last column
states only what remains after that specialization.  In particular, the table
does not treat a change of notation or a standard delta method as a
contribution.

\begingroup
\footnotesize

\label{tab:priority-comparison-main}\\

Prior theorem or formula & Specialization in this manuscript & Residual increment \\

\endfirsthead

(continued).}\\

Prior theorem or formula & Specialization in this manuscript & Residual increment \\

\endlastfoot
Alfa--Neuts D-MAP construction and Zamparo renewal-binary laws and finite-order
Markov criterion \cite{AlfaNeuts1995DMAP,Zamparo2022RenewalBinary}. &
The marked kernel has deterministic post-click state \(D\), so the weak-law
sequence is
\(g_k=e_D^\top T_0^kT_1\mathbf1\) and finite inclusion probabilities factor
as renewal products. &
Theorem~A evaluates \(g_k\), its equal-rate limit, hazard, covariance mode, and
the exact \(1\)-dependence condition for this triangular killed-reset kernel.
These are model-specific consequences, not a general D-MAP theorem. \\
\addlinespace
Ram\'irez-Cobo--Lillo complete weak-equivalence classification for MAP2/MAP3,
together with the MAP2 nonidentifiability analysis
\cite{RamirezCoboLillo2012WeakEquivalentMAP,
RamirezCoboLilloWiper2010MAP2Nonidentifiability}. &
Weak equivalence fixes the scalar sequence
\(e_D^\top T_0^kT_1\mathbf1\).  We intersect its similarity class with
\(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\), \(e_D^\top M=e_D^\top\), the killed marked-reset
constraints, and entrywise Markov nonnegativity. &
\Cref{thm:two-state-complete-fibre-arc-main} is an explicit killed-reset
cone-intersection corollary: the feasible transformations form one closed arc,
its endpoints are the rate-swapped triangular kernels, and it collapses at
equal rates. \\
\addlinespace
M\'esz\'aros--Telek canonical discrete MAP2/RAP2 coordinates and the broader
MAP2 canonical-form literature
\cite{MeszarosTelek2013DiscreteMAP2Canonical,
BodrogHeindlHorvathTelek2008MAP2Canonical}. &
For \(T_0=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p&0\\ b&s\end{smallmatrix}\right)\), the
survival polynomial is
\(x^2-(\operatorname{tr}T_0)x+\det T_0
=x^2-(p+s)x+ps\).  Theorem~B obtains these coefficients from
\((r_0,r_1,r_2)\) through
\((r_2-r_0^2)/(r_1-r_0^2)=p-b\). &
The direct three-inclusion quotient formula and its stability chart are a
candidate original result pending complete reduction against canonical DMAP2
coordinates.  No established priority claim or labelled-kernel inverse is
made. \\
\addlinespace
He--Zhang generalized-Erlang/Coxian representation and minimal Coxian
algorithms \cite{HeZhang2009GeneralizedErlang,HeZhang2008MinimalCoxian}, with
discrete phase-type canonical antecedents
\cite{Cumani1982CanonicalFailure,BobbioEtAl2003AcyclicDPH}. &
For a pure serial sampled kernel, the finite tail is an exponential-polynomial
sequence \(S_k=\sum_j P_j(k)z_j^k\); its annihilating recurrence has roots
\(z_j\), with multiplicities encoded by the polynomial factors \(P_j\). &
\Cref{cor:nstate-serial-all-collision-identifiability-main} is the
finite-coordinate sampled-tail corollary: sufficiently many consecutive tail
coordinates recover the unordered sampled-rate multiset, including repeated
poles.  It is not a general Coxian representation theorem. \\
\addlinespace
Kalman minimal-realization uniqueness and MAP reduction
\cite{Kalman1963LinearSystems,TelekHorvath2007MinimalMAP}. &
Reset-preserving similarities satisfy \(M\mathbf1=\mathbf1\) and
\(e_n^\top M=e_n^\top\); positivity is open at a strictly positive minimal
kernel. &
\Cref{thm:nstate-interior-continuum-nonidentifiability-main} identifies a
local immersed \((n-1)^2\)-dimensional orbit only on the minimal interior
stratum.  No global fibre-dimension statement is inferred. \\
\addlinespace
Bickel--Kwon known-marginal Markov tangent and additive nuisance projection
\cite{BickelKwon2001Semiparametric}. &
The supplementary Markov-gap path uses their double-centred interaction space
and additive projection, then imposes \(q(0,0)=0\) and a
positivity-compatible killed-reset realization. &
Only the added atom constraint, its intersection with the declared path class,
calendar-time scaling, and exchange-local calibration remain model-specific.
The double centring and additive projection are attributed to Bickel--Kwon. \\
\addlinespace
Regenerative CLTs, separated batches, and the multivariate delta method
\cite{Janson2024RenewalCLT,AsmussenGlynn2007,
FlegalJones2010BatchMeansSpectral}. &
Complete retained Palm cycles estimate \((r_0,r_1,r_2)\), after which the
stable quotient map gives \((p+s,ps)\). &
Theorems~C and E discharge the stopped-prefix, plug-in-centering, and quotient
transport details.  The sorted-root and log-rate CLTs are not listed as an
independent innovation. \\
\end{longtable}
\endgroup

\subsection{Sampled-counter interpretation}

Continuous dead-time and exponential-recovery detector models motivate the
two-state parametrization
\cite{YuFessler2000,SarbaziSafariHaas2018LongDeadTime,
KrauseWalenta2025SPADRecovery}.  They do not provide the theorem object here:
continuous within-bin recovery after a click can give a marked transition into
the ready state, whereas the present D-MAP latches every click in the dead
state until the boundary.  The sampled counter is therefore an interpretation
of the constrained kernel, not an independently analysed physical system.

\appendix

\phantomsection\label{app:literature-search-protocol-main}
\paragraph{Supplementary material boundary.}
The supplement contains the stopped separated-batch proof, the Markov--Palm
tangent calculation, the moving-dimensional Helmert coupling, the exact
complete-Palm-gap split subexperiment, the pointwise projection example, and
reproducibility details.  The projection covariance certificate printed below
supports only that supplementary pointwise example at \(z_0=1/2\).
